5 Re-run: no duplicates, no status change, no role removed; edits to names and files update migration-people.test.ts: "changes nothing on a second run, then follows edited names and languages and adds roles only"; "never changes a status: …"; "fails a row whose legacy id is linked to another email, before creating any account". migration-persons.test.ts: "is a no-op the second time". migration-files.test.ts: "changes nothing when run again with the same file, and replaces it when the column names another". data-migration.test.ts: "updates by old id on a second run instead of adding records again".
6 Two organisations with the same legacy ids stay apart (records, people, attachments) migration-people.test.ts: "keeps two organisations with the same legacy ids and emails apart"; "refuses a cross-tenant insert on migration_person_key with a row-level security error". migration-persons.test.ts: "keeps two organisations with the same legacy ids apart". migration-files.test.ts: "keeps the attachments of two organisations apart, even with the same paths". data-migration.test.ts: "needs the migration permission, the import permission of each record type, and stays in its organisation".
7 No email, automation, script notification or receipt comes out of a run; asserted on the mail sender and the outbox Added: migration-acceptance.test.ts: "people and records run with no mail, outbox event, email notice, receipt, invitation or automation, while a normal save of the same type does send them" (mail sender, script.*, connector.* and email outbox rows, email notices, receipts and invitation tokens before and after; automation tag unset; a normal save still enqueues the three script events). Also migration-people.test.ts (mail sender, tokens, notices), data-migration.test.ts: "runs quietly in order, …" (automation off), script-effects.test.ts: "quiet saves enqueue neither script.notify nor connector events, …".
8 Nothing about an account other organisations hold appears in any error, count or Check result migration-people.test.ts: "reuses an existing account silently: same id, password, name, and nothing in the check, errors or counts"; "refuses a globally disabled account that is not a member with the generic wording only"; "reports every problem and warning of plan 3.6 from this organisation alone". No new test was needed.
9 Sources and attachments can be deleted; sensitive values are not kept in samples or failed rows migration-retention.test.ts: "blanks samples of sensitive and personal columns once the mapping is saved, …"; "stores failed rows without the cells of sensitive columns and keeps the rest"; "leaves a sensitive value in no migration_source, job_item or audit row"; "deletes the files, samples and failed cells, and stamps purged_at"; "purges a finished or failed migration 30 days after it finished, and not before". migration-files.test.ts: "deletes unlinked attachments with their bytes and the rows, and leaves files a record holds".
10 Limits shown and enforced; owner-capabilities and layer tests pass; new tables have RLS and are in the export Enforced: migration-files.test.ts: "refuses the 5,001st attachment and a file over the upload limit"; "refuses bad paths, a path used ignoring case, and a running profile"; "refuses an upload past the organisation quota". Added: migration-acceptance.test.ts: "has row-level security and a tenant_isolation policy on every migration table"; "is in the organisation export"; "gives the runtime role only the narrow grants, and no delete on the people map". Also migration-007.test.ts, migration-files.test.ts: "is part of the organisation export and goes with its profile". owner-capabilities.test.ts passes. layer-architecture.test.ts has four failures that name only connector and identity port files, none from the migration code. Weak: "shown" has no screen test (the limits are strings in the Files step); the 50-file and 20,000-row limits are unchanged from before this plan and no test was added for them here.

Weakest coverage: 3 (the same-transaction guarantee is not fault-injected), 10 ("shown"), and the browser journey, which has not been run.

Originally docs/ui-design.md; the Markdown is in docs/deleted/

NGO Platform — app UI design

Status: signed off, 2026-10-23; first-release additions, people, Administration, access and breadcrumb wireframes approved 2026-10-24. Shell chrome updated 2026-10-25 (static app bar, page heading, sign-out on the initials, record groups only on Home and Records). Visuals (illustrative; count badges on Home and Records are not built, see section 13):

  • Step-by-step journeys, phone and desktop side by side: docs/wireframes/ngo-app-navigation-flows.html
  • Earlier whole-app overview (superseded where it differs from this document): legacy/legacy-docs/diagrams/ngo-app-ui-layout.html

This document is the single source for the build. Where the two HTML pages and this document disagree, this document wins.

1. Principles

  1. One set of controls. Phone, tablet and desktop use exactly the same components: app bar, navigation items, chips, search box, Sort and Filters buttons, filter pills, row menu, create button, panels, form fields and buttons. Width changes only the arrangement.
  2. The address is the state. Every screen, tab, filter, sort, scroll depth, open record and form step is in the address. Back, reload, shared links and notifications reproduce the exact screen on any device.
  3. Configuration, not code. Lists, groups, related records, donor copying and forms come from published definitions, editable per organisation.
  4. One logic, many layouts. List behaviour lives in the list session and record behaviour in the record session (framework-free core). Screens only draw.
  5. Nothing loaded from outside. System fonts, the local icon font, no remote assets. The service worker caches the app itself, never record data.

2. Sizes and arrangement

Phone (below 760px) Tablet (760–1099px) Desktop (1100px and up)
Navigation Bottom bar Side rail Side rail
List rows Cards Table, compact columns Table, all columns
Cards/Table switch Hidden (always cards) Shown Shown
Panels Slide up from the bottom, up to 92% of the height Centred, up to 720px wide, single column Centred, up to 720px wide, single column
Create button (+) Above the bottom bar, right Bottom right Bottom right
Builder editors Read-only catalogue Full Full

3. Shell and navigation

  • App bar (all sizes): static. Hardcoded product name NGOOne, the organisation logo when one is available (same-origin path or an embedded image; remote addresses are not loaded), the organisation name, the inbox bell with its badge, and the account initials. The bar does not take the page name. Clicking or hovering the initials opens Sign out. Choosing it shows a warning that this device will be signed out and the person must sign in again, with Cancel and Sign out.
  • Page heading in the content of every screen, left-aligned: the screen name. Back is on that same row, immediately left of the heading, and only off the top-level destinations (Home, Records, Inbox, More). Sign-in, reset password and the print view keep their own titles and have no app bar.
  • Four destinations, in this order: Home, Records, Inbox, More. Same icons and labels in the bottom bar and the side rail.
  • Inbox badge = number of approvals waiting for me. Unread notifications are counted on the Notifications chip only.
  • Record types are no longer listed in the navigation; they are on Home and the Records screen.
  • Breadcrumb (added 2026-10-24) on every route, in one line directly under the app bar:
    • Trail: the destination first (Home, Records, Inbox, More), with no leading "Home" in front of the other destinations (decided 2026-10-24), then:
      • record lists: the record group (from record groups; "Other" when none) and the record type's plural label; the open record's number and title when id is set, or "New " when create=new; and a last item for panel modes where it means something ("Edit", a step label such as "Mark as paid", an action label, "Share", "Import", an opened file);
      • Inbox › Approvals or Notifications;
      • pages: More › (Home pages show Home);
      • More › People (directory), More › My profile, More › Language;
      • More › Administration › People, Roles, Connectors, API keys, Jobs, Audit log or Settings › (for example a person, a role, a connector, "Add a person");
      • Builder › › › (for example Builder › Record types › Donation › Field: Donor name).
    • Examples: Records › Fundraising › Donations › DON/2026-27/00040 · Ravi Kumar › Mark as paid; Inbox › Approvals; More › Administration › Roles › Finance.
    • Links: every item except the last links to its address; going back to a list keeps that list's own chip, search, filters and sort. The last item is plain text and marks the current page.
    • Desktop and tablet: the full trail.
    • Phones: one compact line: the first item, "…", and the last item; tapping "…" opens a small menu with the full trail; a long last item ends with an ellipsis. Back stays on the page heading, left of the title.
    • Labels are translated like all screen text; record titles and definition labels come from the record and the definitions.
    • None on sign-in, reset password and the print view (print layout).
Destination Address Content
Home / Heading "Home", then the greeting; one card per record group with its record types and quick "New …"
Records /records Search box (when more than eight record types), groups with collapsible headers, record types with their descriptions
A record list /records/:type?… See section 5
Inbox /inbox?tab=approvals|notifications See section 7
More /more Account card (initials, name, email; tapping it opens My profile, /more?panel=profile, for everyone; no separate entry), pages, Administration (for administrators), language, two-step sign-in, Builder, About this app. Record groups are not listed here. Sign out is on the account initials, not on this screen.
Administration /admin, /admin/people, /admin/roles, /admin/connectors, /admin/api-keys, /admin/jobs, /admin/audit, /admin/settings People, who has which role, and operational settings, each with its own permission. See section 8b. /people is not a screen and opens Home
Builder /builder, /builder/:kind/:key Catalogue on all sizes; editors on tablet and desktop
Sign-in /sign-in, /reset-password Centred card; buttons stack full width on phones

4. Record groups

  • Shown on Home (one card per group) and on Records (a section header per group, collapsible, remembered per person). Not repeated on More.
  • The Records screen is a single column on every size: groups stack one below the other, as on phones. Wide screens never place groups side by side.
  • New definition kind recordGroup: { key, label, order?, recordTypes[] }. One group per definition, so modules ship their own groups and organisations add or patch groups as usual. Versioned and published like every definition.
  • A record type is in at most one group (the lowest order wins). Record types in no group are listed under Other, so nothing disappears by mistake.
  • Record types a person cannot read are left out; groups left empty are hidden.
  • The Donations module ships Fundraising: Donors, Donations.
  • Builder: a visual "Record groups" editor (add, rename, reorder, remove groups; add or remove record types). Until it is built, the existing JSON editor works for this kind.

5. Record list (every record type)

Top to bottom, the same on every size:

  1. Chips (status views from the list view, e.g. Mine · Outstanding · Closed).
  2. Toolbar: search box, Cards/Table switch (tablet and desktop only), Sort button, Filters button with a count of active filters.
  3. Filter pills, each removable, when filters are active.
  4. Rows: cards on phones; a table on wider screens. Both show the ⋮ row menu (Edit, then the allowed workflow steps) and highlight the record opened last.
  5. Infinite scroll: the next page loads near the end; "Loading more…", then "End of list". A Load more button appears for keyboard users and people who reduce motion.
  6. Create button (+), shown only when the active chip allows create.

Details:

  • Sort: the Sort button opens a menu of sortable columns; on the table, column headers do the same. Each click cycles ascending, descending, cleared; the current direction is shown with ▲ or ▼ in both places.
  • Filters: one Filters panel with the list view's filters (choice lists, yes/no, date ranges, number ranges, text). Reset and Apply. Pills mirror what is applied.
  • Table columns: the list view's columns. Tablet shows compactColumns when the list view sets it; otherwise the title, the first subtitle field and the status.
  • Search runs 300 ms after typing stops.
  • Empty and error states: "Nothing matches this view." with Clear filters; load errors show Retry in place.

6. Record panel (detail, edit, steps, create)

A record always opens in the record panel, on every size and in both list layouts. There is no separate record page any more.

The panel's content is a single column on phones: detail sections, form fields, and the create stepper stack one below the other, exactly as on phones. Wider screens centre the panel (up to 720px wide). A form may lay its fields out in two or three columns on wide screens, but only when its list view (create, edit) or action (form) sets columns; the default is one column. The fields marked "on their own row" take the whole width, and the rest fill the grid left to right in their configured order. A two-column form widens the centred panel to 880px, a three-column form to 1080px. Phones always draw one column, whatever is configured. Detail sections and workflow-step forms stay one column.

Form fields may show a placeholder: grey example text in an empty field (for example ABCDE1234F). It is set per field in the record type (or per action field) and is not stored.

  • Detail: title = the record title; sections from the list view, collapsible:
    • field sections (label/value rows; fields hidden by visibleWhen are left out);
    • receipts;
    • related sections: records of another type that point at this one (e.g. a donor's donations), showing the latest ten, the count, and an optional total of a number or money field. Tapping one opens it in its own list.
  • Pending approval shown at the top of the detail with Approve and Reject (Reject asks for a reason).
  • Footer actions: allowed workflow steps (primary or secondary), then Edit. Step buttons are disabled while an approval is pending.
  • Edit (panel=edit): number and status summary, then the list view's edit fields. Locked fields (editableWhen) are read-only. Cancel / Save.
  • Workflow step (panel=step:<key>): the fields the step requires, then the fields the state it moves to reveals (visibleWhen) and lets the person fill, so a value that belongs to the new state can be entered as the step runs (e.g. a donation's payment reference). All judged against the state it moves to. Cancel / step label.
  • Create (create=new&createStep=<n>): the list view's create steps as a stepper ("Step 1 of 2"), Cancel/Back and Next/Create; or a single form when there are no steps.
  • Fields use the same Material controls everywhere: outlined text, number, email and phone inputs (with the matching phone keyboard), text areas, select, date picker, checkbox, and the record picker for reference fields (section 8).
  • Checks: required fields are checked before sending; server problems appear under the field and in a notice.
  • After saving: the panel returns to the detail view; the row updates in place; a short message appears (e.g. "Mark as paid — receipt RCPT/2026-27/00012 issued."). After create, the new record appears at the top of the list.

7. Inbox

  • Two chips: Approvals (count of items waiting) and Notifications (count of unread). Address /inbox?tab=….
  • Opens on Approvals when anything is waiting, otherwise on Notifications.
  • Approvals work like a record list:

    • Controls: search, Sort, Filters (Type, Step, Requested by) and pills.

    • Rows: cards on phones, a table on tablet and desktop.

    • Only common workflow fields, the same for every record type:

      • Record (number and title; opens the record);
      • Type;
      • Step;
      • Status change (from → to);
      • Requested by;
      • Waiting since;
      • Approvals (given / needed);
      • the Approve and Reject buttons.

      Record-specific fields such as an amount are seen by opening the record.

    • Sort by: Waiting since, Record, Type, Step, Requested by.

    • Order: longest waiting first.

    • Opening a row: shows the record panel with the request at the top, so the whole record can be checked before deciding.

    • Reject: opens a small panel for an optional reason (no browser prompt boxes).

    • Decided requests leave the list.

    • Empty: "Nothing is waiting for you."

    • Address: /inbox?tab=approvals&type=…&step=…&sort=….

    • Server: the approvals inbox needs to return the record title, the step's from and to statuses, and the request time, and to accept search, sort, filters and paging.

  • Notifications: newest first; unread marked New; tapping one marks it read and opens its record; read ones dimmed; Mark all read. Empty: "No notifications."

8. Donors

  • New record type Donor in the Donations module, in the Fundraising group:
    • name (required, searchable);
    • email and phone (searchable);
    • PAN (sensitive);
    • address and city;
    • notes.
  • Donor list view: chip All; rows show name, email and phone; table columns name, email, phone, city. The detail has a Donor section and a related Donations section totalling amount. A "New donation for " action opens create with the donor already chosen.
  • Donation gains a Donor reference field. Its existing donor fields (name, email, phone, PAN) gain copyFrom and editableWhen: "isEmpty(record.donor)":
    • when a donor is chosen, their details are filled in and locked in the form, and the server copies them on save;
    • copying happens only when the chosen donor changes, so a donation keeps the details as they were;
    • with no donor chosen, details can be typed in as today (existing donations keep working unchanged).
  • Record picker (any reference field): type to search the referenced type by its searchable fields; up to eight matches with a second line (email or phone); New "" creates one with the name and selects it; ✕ clears the choice.
  • Permissions: Finance gets record:donor:*; Viewer gets record:donor:read. Choosing a donor requires being able to read donors.
  • New field option copyFrom { reference, field } on any record type: the source field must exist on the referenced type and have a compatible type.

8a. Builder

Screens: docs/wireframes/ngo-builder-screens.html. The Builder uses the same controls as the rest of the app. Editors are for tablet and desktop; phones show the catalogue and published settings read-only.

  • Catalogue:
    • chips per kind with counts, search, Sort, Filters;
    • a table showing the name, kind, key, where each definition comes from (module, organisation, or a module definition the organisation has changed), its state, and the last change;
    • + New for every kind, or start from a ready-made record type (Beneficiary, Expense, Asset).
  • One editor frame for every kind:
    • Visual, JSON, Changes and History tabs;
    • Discard draft, Check, Publish;
    • automatic draft saving.
    • Publish shows a plain-language summary of what changes for people, with warnings.
    • History restores any version as a new draft.
    • Module definitions show "Organisation vs module" changes and offer "Revert to module version".
  • Record type:
    • general details and numbering, with a preview of the next number;
    • a reorderable fields table whose badges summarise each field's rules;
    • a field panel with Basics, Rules, Reference, Copy from a chosen record, Conditions and Calculated;
    • a Rules tab;
    • "Used by", which blocks removing a field while something uses it.
  • Expression editor (new), used by every expression:
    • field and function suggestions;
    • a live check;
    • "Try it" on a real or sample record.
  • Workflow:
    • a diagram drawn from the definition;
    • a states table;
    • a steps table with a step panel: from and to states, fields it asks for, a condition with its message, the permission, and approval (approvers, how many, only when).
  • List view, with a live phone and desktop preview:
    • chips;
    • filters;
    • row layout;
    • table columns and tablet columns;
    • default layout and page size;
    • record panel sections, which can be fields or related records with a total and "New … for this record";
    • the create form (one page or steps, built by dragging fields), chips and prefill;
    • edit fields and chips.
  • Record groups (new):
    • drag to order;
    • one group per record type;
    • an "Other" list;
    • a preview.
  • Templates: subject and message with placeholders, a preview with a real record, and "Send a test to me".
  • Automations:
    • trigger: created, updated, status change, or schedule (shown in plain words);
    • a condition;
    • ordered actions: set fields, run a step, create a record, notify;
    • an on/off switch.
  • Posting and receipt rules: status, condition, date, memo, debit and credit lines, and a balance check on a sample record; series and amount for receipts.
  • Scripts (advanced): code, order and limits; "Try it" in the sandbox; publishing needs the script permission.
  • Theme: colour tokens and radius, a live preview, and a contrast check.
  • The Builder holds only definitions (decided 2026-10-24). People, who has which role, and operational settings (connectors, API keys, jobs, audit log, organisation settings) are in Administration in the app (section 8b). There are no Builder screens for them (decided 2026-10-24).
  • Roles (Builder 18; definitions only):
    • role name and two-step requirement;
    • the permission grid: per record type (see, create, edit, see sensitive, see all, share, import, export, bulk, each workflow step and record action), money and setup permissions (including "See people", people:read, added per role and not to every role by default), and the permission strings the organisation uses on chips, steps, actions, sections, fields, widgets and links;
    • the member count is read-only and links to Administration › Roles; there is no members tab;
    • "What can this role see?" opens the access map's role view.
  • Permissions on parts of screens (decided 2026-10-24). There is no separate permission definition: permissions are the generated ones (record:<type>:…, …:transition:<step>, …:action:<key>, money, setup) plus free-text permission strings. What a role sees is decided element by element:
    • list view chips, workflow steps and record actions: their permission (exists);
    • create and edit: the record type's create and update permissions (exist);
    • record panel sections (list view): a permission; the section is hidden without it (new);
    • fields: readPermission ("Who can see"; "Sensitive" is the same as record:<type>:sensitive), and editPermission ("Who can change"; read-only without it) (new). A field a person may not read shows the same mask as sensitive fields, *** with no partial characters, and that person cannot filter, sort or sum by it (decided 2026-10-24);
    • list toolbar functions: Import, Export, Select and bulk, Share follow their record type permissions (exist);
    • page widgets and link items: a permission, in addition to the page audience (new);
    • app areas: each Administration area (its own permission), Builder (config:read, config:publish), money screens. people:read does not add a screen.
    • Wherever a permission is entered, the Builder offers an autocomplete of known permissions (generated ones plus every permission string already used, with where it is used), still allowing a new one.
  • Access map (Builder 24–25; new): one read-mostly screen listing every element above with the permission it requires and the roles that hold it:
    • filters by area, record type and role, and "Only problems";
    • warnings for permissions no role holds;
    • inline changes for the overridable ones (fields, sections, chips, steps, actions, widgets and links) with the permission autocomplete, saved as drafts of each owning definition and published together (decided 2026-10-24);
    • a role view, "What can Viewer see?", with a plain-language summary and a live preview of the list and record panel as that role sees them.
  • Accounts (there is no person profile record):
    • an account is a member. Adding a person does not create a person record. Name, email and roles belong to the account and are changed in Administration › People;
    • the directory lists active accounts: name, email and roles.
    • My profile (Settings, everyone), the account card:
      • shows the name, email and organisation;
      • name and email are changed by an administrator.
    • Suspending:
      • signs the person out everywhere;
      • the account stays, and the name stays on records, approvals and the audit log;
      • they leave the directory; administrators still see them under the Suspended chip;
      • people are suspended, never deleted.
    • Person field type (person), for any record type, for example "Case worker", "Visited by" or "Fundraiser":
      • chosen with the record picker, which searches active and invited people (not suspended ones) by name or email;
      • stored as the person's user id and shown as their name;
      • lists can filter by it, including "Assigned to me";
      • notifications can go to "the person in field X".
  • Modules: turn on or off, and review updates against the organisation's own changes.

8b. Administration (app; added 2026-10-24)

Wireframes: app Journeys 21 (People directory and Administration) and 23 (operational settings).

  • Where: /admin, from More ("Administration"), on every size, with the same list controls and panels as records. Shown only to people with at least one administration permission; each area inside is shown only with its own permission.
  • People (/admin/people, "Manage people", members:manage): the only people list. /people is not a screen and opens Home.
    • status chips Active, Invited (added, never signed in), Suspended and All; the two-step state; the create button, the row ⋮ and selection;
    • Add a person (?add): email, name, roles, and "Send the invitation email now";
    • a person (?person=<id>): roles, sign-in and invitation state, Resend invitation, Suspend (confirmed in the panel), Reactivate.
  • Roles (/admin/roles, "Manage people" or "Manage roles"): membership only:
    • every role with its member count; a role (/admin/roles/<key>) lists its members with Remove, and the create button adds people (?add);
    • the same assignment is possible from a person's panel;
    • removing the last person who can manage roles is refused;
    • what a role allows is not shown or changed here: "What this role allows" opens Builder 18.
  • Connectors (/admin/connectors, admin:connectors): SMS, WhatsApp and webhooks; what uses each; settings with secrets shown only as set or not set; Test; the delivery log with Retry now. Switch off pauses a connector; Archive retires it (hidden from every choice, kept with its log, listed on the Archived chip) and Restore brings it back switched off.
  • Recycle bin (/admin/recycle-bin, admin:records): records an administrator deleted (Delete is in a record's ⋮ menu), newest first, searchable, with who deleted each and when; Restore puts one back as it was.
  • API keys (/admin/api-keys, admin:api-keys): list with role and last use, create (name, role) with the secret shown once, revoke, link to the API documentation.
  • Background jobs (/admin/jobs, admin:jobs): everyone's imports, bulk operations and rebuilds with progress, failed items and Cancel. People always see their own jobs from their notifications.
  • Audit log (/admin/audit, admin:audit): read-only, who changed what and when, with filters by person (including API keys), area, record type and date.
  • Organisation settings (/admin/settings, admin:settings): name, default language, storage use against the quota and the quota, API requests per minute per key.
  • The permission names admin:audit (audit log) and admin:settings (organisation settings) are final (decided 2026-10-24).
  • Suspending follows section 8a (Accounts, My profile, Suspending).

9. Address contract

Parameter Screen Meaning
view=cards|table Record list Layout on tablet and desktop; ignored on phones
chip, q Record list Chip and search
<field>=a,b, <field>=true|false, <field>From / <field>To, <field>=text Record list Filters
sort=field / sort=-field Record list Sort
show=n Record list Rows loaded (page multiples, at most 500)
filters=open Record list Filters panel open
id, panel=edit|step:<key> Record list Record panel and its mode
create=new, createStep=n, preset parameters (e.g. donor) Record list Create panel
tab=approvals|notifications Inbox Inbox tab
status, role, q, sort, filter parameters, person, add Administration › People (/admin/people) Status chip, filters, search, sort, the open person, the Add a person panel
/admin/roles/:key, add Administration › Roles The open role, the Add people panel
/admin/connectors/:key, status, job, filter parameters Administration › Connectors, Background jobs, Audit log The open connector, job status chip, the open job, filters
panel=profile|language More My profile panel, language panel

History: changing chip, search, filters, sort, depth or layout replaces the current entry and returns to the first page; opening or closing a record or create, changing panel mode or create step, and switching Inbox tab add one, so Back steps back through them. /records/:type/:id and /records/:type/new redirect into this pattern.

Breadcrumb: derived entirely from the address and the definitions, with no parameters of its own; reloading or sharing a link reproduces the same trail.

10. Installable and store app

  • Installable web app (now):
    • manifest with name, icons and brand colour, opening in its own window;
    • a service worker caching the app shell only;
    • an offline banner showing the last loaded list, with changes blocked;
    • a "A new version is ready — Reload" message;
    • safe-area padding.
  • Known bug — installed icon (2026-11-03). The installed shortcut should use the organisation logo when one is set, and the shared heart when it is not. On Windows, Chrome still draws a letter (the first letter of the organisation name) on the shortcut and the taskbar. The icon file itself is a square PNG of the logo, served as image/png with a cacheable response, and a changed icon address still does not replace the letter after the app is removed and installed again. The app name and the sign-in page it opens are unaffected. A WebP logo is not drawn into that square and falls back to the heart.
  • Store apps (later, Capacitor):
    • the same build;
    • a configurable API address and mobile sign-in with the token in secure storage;
    • hardware Back mapped to the address history;
    • push notifications opening the right screen;
    • camera and share sheet.
  • Offline capture (later): saves queued on the device and sent when online.

11. Accessibility and touch

  • Touch targets at least 44px on touch screens.
  • Visible focus everywhere; Enter opens a focused row.
  • Panels trap focus and return it to the row on close.
  • Reduced motion turns off slide animations and shows Load more.
  • Colour is never the only signal; status badges carry text.
  • The breadcrumb is a navigation landmark labelled "Breadcrumb"; its last item carries aria-current="page"; the phone "…" button has an accessible name ("Show the full trail") and its menu is reachable by keyboard.

12. What goes away

  • Table layout's own controls: its toolbar, status tabs and inline filter card. It now uses the shared controls.
  • Record page: the full-page record form; records always open in the record panel.
  • Old navigation: record types listed in the side navigation, and the top bar's Inbox link and Sign out button. Record types move to Home and Records. Sign out moves to the account initials, with a confirmation.
  • Inbox: the desktop approvals table and the browser prompt box for reject reasons.

13. Decisions (signed off 2026-10-23)

  1. No record counts on Home or the Records screen: record types are listed by name only. The count badges in the HTML mockups are not built.
  2. compactColumns is added to the list view as an optional setting for tablet tables. Without it, tablets show the title, the first subtitle field, and the status.
  3. Existing donations are not linked to donors automatically. A tool that suggests matches by email or phone may come later.
  4. Record groups get a visual editor in the Builder in this build.
  5. Record panel is one column on phones, and up to three columns on the web only where a form's list view or action asks for them (section 6).
  6. Records screen is single column on the web too: groups stack, never side by side (section 4).

13a. First release additions (2026-10-23)

Everything below ships in the first release. The architecture is in docs/documents.html#architecture §5.6, §6.2 and §6.7–6.10 (server) and §10.6–10.8 (web app). Wireframes for the new screens are still to be drawn.

  1. Fast lists without migrations: indexes for references, unique fields, and filtered or sorted fields are created automatically when definitions are published. Search is fast on large lists.

  2. Languages: English, Hindi, Tamil, Telugu, Bengali and Marathi for all screen text. Organisations can translate their own labels and messages in the Builder (Translations grid). Each person chooses a language in More.

  3. Record actions: organisations add buttons to the record panel and the row menu, for example Send reminder, Pay now and Print receipt. An action can ask for a few fields first, and runs steps such as setting fields, running a workflow step, notifying people, calling a webhook, creating a payment, or opening the receipt print view.

  4. Pages and pinned views:

    • Home is a configurable page with counts, totals, charts, pinned lists, links and the record groups.
    • Extra pages appear in More.
    • Pinning: anyone can pin a list to their Home from the list's ⋮ menu, and administrators can pin it for everyone.
    • Counts: the record-type lists on Home and Records still show no counts; counts appear only in configured widgets.

  5. Visual editors for everything: every kind of setting gets a visual editor generated from its rules, including new kinds. Hand-built editors remain for record types, workflows, list views, pages and translations.

  6. Connectors: SMS, WhatsApp and outgoing webhooks, configured and switched on per organisation in Administration (section 8b), with a test button and a delivery log. Nothing is sent unless configured.

  7. Field options:

    • unique values, for example one donor per PAN;
    • format rules with a message;
    • multiple choice;
    • totals kept on a record, for example a donor's total given and number of donations.

  8. Public API and import/export:

    • API keys per organisation, each acting with a chosen role, created and revoked in Administration (section 8b).
    • Documentation for the API is generated from the organisation's own setup.
    • CSV import from any record list: upload, match columns to fields, check every row before anything is saved, run, then see what failed and download it.
    • Create or update: an import can update existing records that match a unique field.
    • CSV export of the current list or the selected rows.

  9. Attachments: a file field for photos, PDFs, documents and spreadsheets.

    • Adding files: choose, drag and drop, or take a photo on a phone.
    • Viewing: images and PDFs preview in the record panel.
    • Storage: files are stored outside the database, with a storage limit per organisation.

  10. Record sharing: a record type can be set to "only their own and shared".

    • What people see: their own records, records shared with them or their role, and everything only if allowed.
    • Sharing: from the record panel, with read or edit access and an optional end date.
    • List chip: a "Shared with me" chip appears automatically.

  11. Bulk actions: select rows (checkboxes in the table, long-press or Select on cards), or "all that match". Then, from the bulk bar:

    • run a workflow step or a bulk action;
    • set fields;
    • share;
    • export.

    Large runs continue in the background with progress and a notification when done.

  12. New field types:

    • Location: "Use my location" or typed coordinates, with a label. Lists can filter by "within X km". There are no maps inside the app; an optional "Open in maps" link is shown.
    • Signature: sign with a finger, mouse or pen. It records who signed and when, and appears on the print view.
    • Rich text: bold, italic, lists, headings, quotes and links. Text is cleaned of anything unsafe.

  13. More than one workflow per record type:

    • Alternatives: records can follow one of several workflows, chosen when they are created (for example by programme).
    • Parallel: a record can run a second workflow at the same time (for example case closure beside intake).
    • Record panel: shows each workflow's state, with its steps grouped by workflow.

  14. People and profiles (added 2026-10-24):

    • Administration in the app at /admin, from More, for administrators: People (adding, invitations, roles, suspending), Roles (who has which role), connectors, API keys, background jobs, the audit log and organisation settings. The Builder keeps only definitions. People are not a destination in the bar or in More.
    • Fine-grained visibility: besides chips, steps and actions, record panel sections, fields ("Who can see", "Who can change"), page widgets and links can each require a permission; the Builder's access map shows what each part requires and which roles hold it.
    • Profiles: one profile per person (record type person), with fields the organisation adds; each field can be "changeable by the person" and "shown in the directory".
    • My profile for everyone, opened from their name at the top of More, changing only the fields they are allowed to.
    • Person fields on any record type (for example "Case worker"), with "Assigned to me" filters and notifications to that person.
    • Details in sections 8a (Builder: roles, permissions on parts of screens, access map, profile fields) and 8b (Administration).

The four navigation destinations (Home, Records, Inbox, More) stay as designed.

14. Build order (after sign-off)

Superseded by the twelve phases in the architecture document (§7), which include the first-release additions. The list below is kept for history.

  1. Backend: the recordGroup kind, copyFrom, related sections, and the Donor definitions and permissions, with tests.
  2. Shell: the app bar, bottom bar and side rail, and the Records, Home and More screens with groups.
  3. One records screen: the shared controls and record panel on every size; the table becomes rows only; the record page is removed (with approval for the deletions).
  4. Inbox: the Approvals and Notifications tabs, the approval cards, and the reject panel.
  5. Record picker: the donor picker, "New donor", and the related Donations section.
  6. Installable web app.
  7. Finish: full tests, architecture doc update, local commit.

Originally docs/deployment.md; the Markdown is in docs/deleted/

Deployment and operations

One document for how OneNGO is deployed, run and checked. The workflow files in .github/workflows/ and the scripts in .github/scripts/ are the source of truth for each step. This page also holds the former pipeline setup guide (sections 6 and 7) and the monitoring README (section 8). The longer earlier texts are kept in docs/deleted/ (operations/deployment-plan.html, operations/devops-handover.md, operations/pipeline-setup.md, monitoring/README.md); they are out of date in places, so trust this page and the workflow files first.

Never paste a secret into a document, a commit, a pipeline file or a ticket.

1. Shape

Question Decision
Production App Engine standard, F1, asia-south1. Two services: default (organisation app, apps/onengo/server) and console (operator console, apps/onengo-console/server). Max 1 instance each, min 0
Stage Cloud Run (ngo, ngo-console) in its own Google Cloud project, deployed from develop
Pipeline GitHub Actions. main deploys production. develop runs the gate and deploys stage
Credentials Workload identity federation. Production trusts only refs/heads/main, stage only develop. No key files
Console access Behind Google Identity-Aware Proxy: the console verifies the IAP token against CONSOLE_IAP_AUDIENCE (passed as OPERATOR_IAP_AUDIENCE, required outside local mode), so only /health answers without a sign-in. Getting in also needs an enabled console account and a fresh two-factor sign-in (required again for suspend, reactivate, close). An earlier version of this page said "no IAP"; the code, manifest and smoke test say otherwise
Database One PostgreSQL 15+ database outside Google Cloud. Five connection strings with different logins (below). Row-level security separates organisations
Secrets GitHub environment secrets. Production: the deploy writes each service's settings to one JSON secret in Secret Manager (ngo-prod-config, ngo-console-prod-config) and dist/bootstrap.js reads it at start-up; secret values are never in app.yaml or the repository. Stage: one Secret Manager secret per setting (ngo-*). If Secret Manager is unreachable new instances fail to start; running ones keep working
Release safety Deploy with no traffic, curl /health, move traffic, curl the public address, move traffic back on failure

Hosts: https://<project>.<region>.r.appspot.com and https://console-dot-<project>.<region>.r.appspot.com. A custom domain changes PUBLIC_BASE_URL and the redirect addresses registered with the identity provider and each connector.

One database, four serving logins. DATABASE_OWNER_URL (migrations, deploy job only), SYSTEM_DATABASE_URL and RUNTIME_DATABASE_URL (main), CONSOLE_DATABASE_URL and CONSOLE_RUNTIME_URL (console). Pointing two of them at one login defeats the separation.

Cost. App Engine gives 28 F1 hours a day per project. max_instances: 1 matters because a candidate version runs beside the live one during every deploy. Watch the quota page in the first week. Cleanup (section 5) keeps stored versions small.

2. Server settings

The server reads environment variables (commented in .env.example) and never reads .env in production.

Variable Required Notes
DATABASE_URL yes PostgreSQL 15 or later only. Another scheme, MySQL, or an older server logs Start-up failed and exits 1 before migrating. Use the provider's pooled TLS string
INSTALLATION_MODE no one-ngo (default) or shared. Anything else stops start-up
DATABASE_POOL_SIZE no Default 3
TOKEN_SECRET yes 32+ characters. Changing it signs everyone out
ENCRYPTION_KEY yes 32 random bytes, base64. Encrypts two-step secrets, connector and gateway credentials, sensitive record values. Losing it makes them unreadable unless the old key is in ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS
ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS no Earlier keys, comma separated. After a rotation run npm run fields:reencrypt, and drop the old key once its jobs are done
PUBLIC_BASE_URL yes Address people use. Used in emailed links, redirects and webhook URLs
SHIPPED_MODULES no Default donations
ONENGO_PRODUCT_NAME no Name shown in sign-in, the app bar and browser titles. Unset or empty means OneNGO; any other value, including a space, is shown exactly as given
TICK_SECRET no Only for a scheduler other than App Engine cron (POST /internal/tick, bearer)
MEMORY_ALERT_MB no Default 300. Threshold for the memory.high alert
AUDIT_ARCHIVE_BUCKET no Bucket for the operator audit archive
SMTP_* no Without them, sign-in codes and invitations are not sent
OIDC_CLIENT_ID, OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET, OIDC_ISSUER, OIDC_ALLOWED_DOMAINS, OIDC_PROVIDER_ID, OIDC_PROVIDER_LABEL no Shared OpenID Connect provider each NGO may opt into. Empty issuer means Google. Redirect URI <PUBLIC_BASE_URL>/api/auth/oidc/callback. Keep OIDC_PROVIDER_ID stable once NGOs opt in. Old GOOGLE_* names are deprecated
CONNECTOR_ALLOWED_HOSTS no Hosts connectors may call even if non-public. Empty allows none. Connectors use https:// only and never follow redirects
BREACHED_PASSWORD_CHECK no online (default) or off
TRUST_PROXY no Reverse-proxy hops to trust for X-Forwarded-For: a number or list of addresses. Unset trusts none; App Engine defaults to 1. true and * are refused. IPv6 callers group by /64

Console and sign-in. Operators with the sign_in grant set each organisation's sign-in policy, shared-provider opt-in and own OpenID providers, with a fresh step-up and a reason, audited without secrets. Provider secrets live in connector.secret_enc under ENCRYPTION_KEY, so the console gets the main ENCRYPTION_KEY (and _PREVIOUS during rotation) beside its own OPERATOR_ENCRYPTION_KEY. It reads the non-secret OIDC_* values and never OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET. The first operator is inserted in SQL (local development: utilities/seed/console-operator.sql). In production the database administrator creates the first operator once with utilities/access/create-first-operator.sql, using the issuer and subject the console shows when it refuses a sign-in (see Developer guide, first console account).

OAuth connectors. An organisation's administrator registers the redirect <PUBLIC_BASE_URL>/api/connectors/oauth/callback with the provider, so PUBLIC_BASE_URL must be exact. Losing the key means reconnecting every oauth connector.

GitHub environment variables (set in each of prod and stage at environment level, never at repository level, or stage and production would share one value, including the Google Cloud project). Not secret, but kept per environment.

Variable Used by What it is
GCP_PROJECT_ID all deploys, cleanup, rollback The project's ID
GCP_REGION stage, rollback Optional. Defaults to asia-south1
GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER all Full name of the Workload Identity provider
GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT all The deploy service account's email
SERVICE_ACCOUNT all The main runtime service account's email
CONSOLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT all The console runtime service account's email (different from the main one)
PUBLIC_BASE_URL all The main app's public address, for example https://app.example.org
CONSOLE_PUBLIC_BASE_URL all The console's public address
CONSOLE_OIDC_ISSUER, CONSOLE_OIDC_CLIENT_ID, CONSOLE_OIDC_AUDIENCE all The console's sign-in provider: issuer, client ID, and the audience the console expects in sign-in tokens
CONSOLE_IAP_AUDIENCE all The Identity-Aware Proxy audience for the console (OPERATOR_IAP_AUDIENCE)

Browser tests only (e2e-test.yml), optional: E2E_ORG, E2E_CONSOLE, CONSOLE_E2E_OPERATOR.

GitHub environment secrets (each of prod and stage). Never paste a value into a file, an issue or a chat. Do not reuse a value between main and console.

Secret Used for
DATABASE_OWNER_URL Owner login for migrations (npm run deploy:prepare). Not used at runtime
SYSTEM_DATABASE_URL, RUNTIME_DATABASE_URL Main server's logins: system, and non-owner for tenant traffic
CONSOLE_DATABASE_URL, CONSOLE_RUNTIME_URL Console's control-plane and runtime logins
TOKEN_SECRET Signs session tokens (openssl rand -hex 32). Rotating it signs everyone out
ENCRYPTION_KEY Encrypts stored sensitive values; shared by both servers. Back it up apart from GitHub, with its version history: losing it makes stored values unreadable. Rotating it is a separate procedure (ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS, npm run fields:reencrypt)
CONSOLE_ENCRYPTION_KEY The console's own session encryption (a different key)
CONSOLE_INTERNAL_TOKEN_SECRET Signs internal console tokens
CONSOLE_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET Console sign-in client secret, needed if the identity provider client is confidential
TICK_SECRET Stage only: bearer secret for the hourly /internal/tick. Production uses App Engine cron
E2E_PASSWORD Optional: seeded test member password for e2e-test.yml

A value required for production must be non-empty: the store step refuses to write an empty one and fails the run. SMTP, gateway and connector credentials are optional; organisations set their own gateway and mail in the app.

3. Releasing

On the build machine or in CI:

npm ci
npm --prefix web ci
npm run build:server        # dist/server.js and dist/deploy.js
npm run web:build           # web/dist/web/browser
$env:DATABASE_URL = '<target database>'
npm run deploy:prepare      # migrations, platform package, organisation re-sync, profiles, shipped modules
gcloud app deploy           # only if deploy:prepare succeeded
  • The deploy step runs once per release from one place, before the new version starts. Running it again is harmless.
  • The server never migrates. If the deploy step did not run, the new version refuses to start and the old one keeps serving.
  • Every migration must work with the release before it, because moving traffic back cannot undo one. Drop or rename a column only in a later release than the one that stopped using it.
  • Migrations are the seven files 001_core.sql to 007_bank_reconciliation.sql in packages/platform/migrations/. Before the first release they are kept in final-state form (change the file that owns the table). From the first release on, do not rename or renumber an applied file; its name is its history row. New changes go in new files after the last one.
  • npm run fields:reencrypt re-encrypts stored secrets and queues one fields.reencrypt job per record type. Run it once after the first deploy that includes field encryption and once after every key rotation. A secret no configured key opens fails the run and changes nothing.

Scheduler. App Engine cron calls GET /internal/tick (accepted only with the X-Appengine-Cron header on App Engine), from cron.yaml: every 60 minutes for a one-NGO installation, every 5 for a shared one. Deploy it with gcloud app deploy cron.yaml. Elsewhere, Cloud Scheduler calls POST /internal/tick with Authorization: Bearer <TICK_SECRET>; keep the secret in Secret Manager and restrict who can read the job.

4. Pipeline

Workflow When What
ci.yml Every pull request Two parallel jobs and a CI gate that fails unless both pass: typecheck, build and every vitest test against a throwaway postgres:16 (including the artifact test that starts the real bundle and checks two clean builds match); and npm run ci:audit (high-severity production dependency findings). Deploys nothing
deploy.yml Push to develop or main Runs the gate again, works out the release version from package.json, calls the deploy workflow below, and pushes the release tag only after that deploy succeeds
deploy-cloudrun.yml After the gate on develop Builds both servers, migrates the stage database, syncs the stage secrets to Secret Manager, deploys ngo and ngo-console (512 MiB, 1 CPU, min 0, max 1), tags the revision candidate, moves traffic once /health answers, checks the public addresses, returns traffic on failure. Cloud Scheduler posts /internal/tick hourly
deploy-gcp.yml After the gate on main, or a manual run on main Required reviewer on prod; build from the exact commit; fill the non-secret REPLACE_WITH_ placeholders in app.yaml (a missing one stops here, before anything changes); record the serving versions; deploy:prepare with the owner URL; store-prod-config.mjs writes each service's settings from GitHub secrets to its Secret Manager secret over stdin (never printed; the two newest versions are kept; the runtime account gets secretAccessor on its own secret); deploy both services --no-promote as v<tag>-r<run>; .github/scripts/smoke-test.mjs checks each new version (main: /health {"status":"ok"}, /ready {"status":"ready"} which proves the database answers, and /sign-in; console, behind IAP: /health only); move traffic; curl the public addresses; roll back on failure; deploy cron.yaml. At start-up dist/bootstrap.js reads its secret, puts the values in the environment and starts the server; a missing secret, or a setting app.yaml still sets, stops the instance before it serves traffic
rollback.yml Manual run Moves traffic back to an earlier version or revision (see below)
e2e-test.yml Manual run only Browser (Playwright) tests against a deployed environment. Choose the target (stage or prod) and the suite (full or selected), and optionally a title filter or an address. See below
cleanup.yml See section 5

Tags. Every deploy is of a tag from version in the root package.json: develop gets <version>-develop.<n>, main gets <version>. Raise version before the next merge; a merge that repeats a tagged version stops at the tag job. A re-run on a tagged commit reuses its tag. The tag is pushed only after the deploy succeeds, so a failed deploy leaves no tag. Production only ships what went through stage: the tag job refuses a production release unless a <version>-develop.N tag is in the commit's history, so merge develop into main with a merge commit or fast-forward, not a squash. App Engine versions are named v<tag with dots as hyphens>-r<run> and Cloud Run revisions end in the same.

Rollback (rollback.yml). Run it by hand, choose stage or prod, and give the previous App Engine version (prod) or Cloud Run revision (stage) names for the main service and the console; the deploy summary lists them. The target environment's reviewers approve first. It moves traffic only and does not undo migrations, which is why every migration must work with the release before it. The first-ever deploy has no earlier version, so there is nothing to roll back to.

Gates that block a merge or deploy: typecheck; all builds (both servers, both web apps, the guide); unit, PostgreSQL and row-level-security tests; the bundle test; the console manifest test (the console stays service: console with no IAP, so nobody quietly reverts the access model); the dependency audit; the prod approval; the placeholder check.

Browser tests (e2e-test.yml). No deploy starts them; run the workflow from the Actions tab when you want them. The tests come from the branch or tag chosen in "Use workflow from". Addresses come from the target environment's PUBLIC_BASE_URL and CONSOLE_PUBLIC_BASE_URL unless you type one in.

Suite What it runs Where
selected tests/e2e/post-deploy: opens the app and console sign-in pages, signs in nothing, writes nothing stage or prod
full Everything in tests/e2e/specs: signs in as the seeded Riverbank members and creates donors, donations and API keys stage only; the workflow refuses it on prod. The stage database needs the Riverbank seed (utilities/data/dev-seed.sql)

Specs that need something stage lacks (a mailbox, the payment fixture) skip themselves with a reason. Optional settings on the stage environment: variable E2E_ORG and secret E2E_PASSWORD (the seeded organisation and member password; defaults riverbank and Password@01), and variable E2E_CONSOLE=true with CONSOLE_E2E_OPERATOR to include the operator console journeys. A prod run waits for the prod reviewers like a deploy does. The report is kept as an artifact for seven days.

5. Cleanup

cleanup.yml is separate from releases. Mon, Wed, Fri 17:00 UTC it runs as a dry run and reports what it would delete. To delete, run it from Actions with dry run unticked. Both need a prod reviewer. It keeps every App Engine version that has traffic plus the newest five per service (a version whose traffic split cannot be read is kept), deletes staging-bucket files older than three days, and deletes untagged Artifact Registry images. The schedule only fires once the file is on the default branch.

6. One-time setup

Database (PostgreSQL 15+ with pg_trgm). The serving logins can only be granted group roles that the first deploy:prepare creates, so do this once by hand:

npm ci
npm run build:server
$env:DATABASE_OWNER_URL = "postgres://owner:...@host/db?sslmode=require"
npm run deploy:prepare

create role ngo_main_system_login      login password '<generated>';  grant ngo_main_system      to ngo_main_system_login;
create role ngo_main_runtime_login     login password '<generated>';  grant ngo_main_runtime     to ngo_main_runtime_login;
create role ngo_operator_login         login password '<generated>';  grant ngo_operator         to ngo_operator_login;
create role ngo_operator_runtime_login login password '<generated>';  grant ngo_operator_runtime to ngo_operator_runtime_login;

Build the four connection strings from these logins (add ?sslmode=require if needed) and store them as the four database secrets.

Google Cloud (production)

$PROJECT = "your-project-id"
gcloud config set project $PROJECT
gcloud services enable appengine.googleapis.com cloudbuild.googleapis.com cloudscheduler.googleapis.com iamcredentials.googleapis.com iam.googleapis.com cloudresourcemanager.googleapis.com logging.googleapis.com secretmanager.googleapis.com artifactregistry.googleapis.com storage.googleapis.com
gcloud app create --region=asia-south1        # permanent
gcloud iam service-accounts create ngo-main    --display-name "NGO main runtime"
gcloud iam service-accounts create ngo-console --display-name "NGO console runtime"
# each runtime account needs only roles/logging.logWriter

$REPO = "OWNER/REPO"
$NUMBER = gcloud projects describe $PROJECT --format="value(projectNumber)"
gcloud iam service-accounts create gha-deploy --display-name "GitHub Actions deploy"
gcloud iam workload-identity-pools create github --location=global
gcloud iam workload-identity-pools providers create-oidc github-repo `
  --location=global --workload-identity-pool=github `
  --issuer-uri="https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com" `
  --attribute-mapping="google.subject=assertion.sub,attribute.repository=assertion.repository,attribute.ref=assertion.ref" `
  --attribute-condition="assertion.repository=='$REPO' && assertion.ref=='refs/heads/main'"
gcloud iam service-accounts add-iam-policy-binding "gha-deploy@$PROJECT.iam.gserviceaccount.com" `
  --role roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser `
  --member "principalSet://iam.googleapis.com/projects/$NUMBER/locations/global/workloadIdentityPools/github/attribute.repository/$REPO"

Deploy account roles (a starting set; tighten after the first run, since a permission error names the missing role): appengine.deployer, appengine.serviceAdmin, appengine.appViewer, cloudbuild.builds.editor, storage.objectAdmin on the App Engine staging buckets, cloudscheduler.admin, iam.serviceAccountUser on both runtime accounts, and artifactregistry.repoAdmin only if a dry run reports images. Do not give appengine.appCreator. The production store step also needs Secret Manager access (secretmanager.admin was the role used to write secrets and bind the runtime accounts), and stage needs run.admin; an earlier guide listed appengine.appAdmin and storage.admin instead of the narrower roles above. Use three accounts: the deploy account (used by GitHub), the main runtime account and the console runtime account, and give the deploy account iam.serviceAccountUser on the two runtime accounts. Create the production App Engine app once (gcloud app create --region=asia-south1); the production workflow stops if it does not exist. Production and stage must be separate projects. The runtime accounts never need a key file: they read their secrets with their own identity.

Stage. A second project (so stage does not use production's App Engine hours) with Cloud Run, Cloud Build, Secret Manager, Cloud Scheduler and IAM Credentials enabled, two runtime accounts and a deploy account whose identity trusts only develop. Its own database and four logins, never production's. Leave PUBLIC_BASE_URL and CONSOLE_PUBLIC_BASE_URL empty on the first run (the health check uses the Cloud Run addresses), then copy the URLs from the deploy summary into them and run again before anyone signs in, because the first revision advertises https://pending.invalid.

GitHub. Add the remote and push. Create environment prod with a required reviewer, deployment branches limited to main, and the variables and secrets above. Create stage with no reviewer, limited to develop. Protect main and develop: require a pull request and the single CI gate check (not the individual jobs, so later jobs join the gate without changing the rule). Set workflow permissions to read-only by default. Consider pinning actions to commit hashes with Dependabot.

Staging for O1 and O6 needs a staging service in asia-south1 and a staging PostgreSQL (Mumbai where offered), loaded with test data only.

What the pipeline creates in Google Cloud (you do not create these by hand):

Where Name Created by Contents
Production Secret Manager ngo-prod-config deploy-gcp.yml store step JSON of the main server's settings
Production Secret Manager ngo-console-prod-config deploy-gcp.yml store step JSON of the console's settings
Stage Secret Manager ngo-* secrets sync-stage-secrets.sh One secret per setting
Stage Cloud Scheduler ngo-tick deploy-cloudrun.yml Hourly call to /internal/tick

First run. (1) Create the two projects, service accounts and workload identity. (2) Create stage and prod with the protection rules above. (3) Set every variable and secret in both. (4) Create the App Engine app in production. (5) Merge to develop; confirm the stage deploy succeeds and the <version>-develop.1 tag appears. (6) Merge develop into main with a merge commit and approve the prod run. (7) Confirm the new version holds no secret values: gcloud app versions describe <version> --service=default --project <project>; its env_variables must not contain database URLs, TOKEN_SECRET or ENCRYPTION_KEY. (8) Run e2e-test.yml against stage with suite selected.

Before the first production deploy. Delete any App Engine versions deployed before the Secret Manager change: they still hold the old plaintext values. Rotate the production database passwords and TOKEN_SECRET (rotating TOKEN_SECRET signs everyone out; rotating ENCRYPTION_KEY is separate, because stored values are encrypted with it). Limit who can read Secret Manager on the production project.

Troubleshooting.

Symptom Likely cause
Set <NAME> to a PostgreSQL connection string. The database secret is missing or empty in that environment
Remove these from app.yaml; they now come from Secret Manager A setting is still in app.yaml; remove it
Secret Manager refused ngo-prod-config with status 403 The runtime account lacks secretAccessor on the secret, or the store step did not run
Create the App Engine app once: gcloud app create ... The App Engine app does not exist in the production project
Release X is already tagged on another commit Raise version in package.json before the next release
No <version>-develop.N tag is in this commit's history The release has not gone through stage; deploy it from develop first
Production instances fail to start Read the start-up log for Start-up failed:; it names the setting that is wrong, never its value

7. Go-live checklist

  1. Merge the branch into develop, then develop into main once the gate is green on a pull request.
  2. Create the Google Cloud project, App Engine app, service accounts and workload identity.
  3. Create the database, run the first deploy:prepare, create the four logins.
  4. Create prod and add the variables and secrets.
  5. Protect main and develop with the CI gate.
  6. Register the public addresses with the identity provider (console redirect /op/auth/callback), Google sign-in (/api/auth/oidc/callback) if used, and connectors (/api/connectors/oauth/callback).
  7. Push to main, approve, read the deploy summary and write down the release name.
  8. Create the first console account (Developer guide), after the console is deployed.
  9. Rehearse a failed release once, before real data is in: deploy a build whose /health does not answer and watch traffic return.
  10. Run Cleanup (production) once as a dry run and read the report before deleting anything.
  11. Complete alerts (section 8) and the backup and restore drill (section 9).

Not yet verified (the first runs will show): Playwright against a live address, npm run ci on a GitHub runner, the gcloud flags and deploy account roles against a live project, automatic rollback in practice, and the stage Cloud Run deploy.

8. Alerts and dashboard

The server writes one JSON log line per event to stdout (Cloud Logging). Alerts watch a fixed alert field.

alert Severity When First response
outbox.failed_permanently ERROR A background event (email, SMS, webhook, automation, payment step) failed its fifth attempt Read eventType, tenantId and the error; usually a connector setting. To retry, set that outbox_event row to pending with attempts 0
job.failed ERROR A background job stopped with an error Read kind, jobId and the error. The person who started it can retry once fixed
http.unhandled_error ERROR A 500 from an unexpected error. The policy fires above 5 in 5 minutes Search the request id in the entry's trace. A burst after a deploy points at the release: route traffic back
memory.high WARNING Resident memory above MEMORY_ALERT_MB (default 300 of the F1's 384 MB) Look for a large import or export, or growth since a deploy; steady load needs a larger instance class

Nothing here is applied automatically. No build, test, deploy step or server process reads the definitions. A person with Logging Admin and Monitoring Editor on the project applies them once with gcloud (alpha and beta components needed), and again after a file changes. The definitions are one log-based metric per alert (the file name is the metric name), one policy per metric (the HTTP one is the error-rate policy), and the "NGO platform" dashboard (the four metrics, responses by status code, instance memory). Their full text is in section 8.1 below. Save each block under the file name in its heading, in a working folder monitoring/ with sub-folders metrics/ and policies/ (the folder is not part of the repository), then run the commands. Policy files carry no notification channel; choose it with the product owner and pass it at create time. Apply metrics first, then policies, then the dashboard:

$project = 'my-ngo-project'
Get-ChildItem monitoring/metrics/*.json | ForEach-Object {
  gcloud logging metrics create $_.BaseName --project=$project --config-from-file=$_.FullName
}
gcloud beta monitoring channels list --project=$project     # find or create the channel first
$channel = "projects/$project/notificationChannels/CHANNEL_ID"
Get-ChildItem monitoring/policies/*.json | ForEach-Object {
  gcloud alpha monitoring policies create --project=$project --policy-from-file=$_.FullName --notification-channels=$channel
}
gcloud monitoring dashboards create --project=$project --config-from-file=monitoring/dashboard.json

Do not run create twice (it makes a second policy or dashboard). To change one use gcloud logging metrics update <name> --config-from-file=..., gcloud alpha monitoring policies update <policy id> --policy-from-file=... or gcloud monitoring dashboards update <dashboard id> --config-from-file=.... Thresholds in the files: the job-failed, memory-high and outbox policies fire on any value above 0 within 5 minutes; the HTTP policy fires above 5 in 5 minutes. On the dashboard, appengine.googleapis.com/system/memory/usage is App Engine's own measurement summed over a version's instances, whereas memory.high is the process's own reading per instance; the dashboard's memory line is fixed at 300 MiB and does not follow MEMORY_ALERT_MB.

Check that an alert fires by writing a test entry (Bash, not PowerShell, which mangles the JSON):

gcloud logging write ngo-alert-check '{"alert":"job.failed","kind":"alert-check","message":"Alert check"}' \
  --project=<project id> --payload-type=json --severity=ERROR \
  --monitored-resource-type=gae_app --monitored-resource-labels=module_id=default,version_id=alert-check

An incident should open within minutes and close itself 30 minutes after the last entry. Repeat with memory.high at WARNING. Tune the error-rate threshold after a week of real traffic. The policies and dashboard filter on resource.type="gae_app"; on another host each needs that changed. The files were checked only as valid JSON; send any gcloud rejection to the development team rather than editing in place.

8.1 Definition files

These nine files were kept in a monitoring folder under docs and are reproduced here unchanged except for the pointer in each metric description. Four metrics, four policies and one dashboard. All filters use resource.type="gae_app", so they cover production on App Engine but not stage on Cloud Run.

monitoring/metrics/ngo_http_unhandled_error.json

{
  "description": "NGO platform: requests answered 500 because of an unexpected error. Applied by a person with gcloud; see docs/documents.html#deployment, section 8.",
  "filter": "jsonPayload.alert=\"http.unhandled_error\"",
  "metricDescriptor": {
    "metricKind": "DELTA",
    "valueType": "INT64",
    "unit": "1",
    "labels": [{ "key": "method", "valueType": "STRING", "description": "HTTP method of the failed request." }]
  },
  "labelExtractors": { "method": "EXTRACT(jsonPayload.method)" }
}

9. Backup and restore drill

Goal: prove the hosted database restores into a fresh one, the platform runs on the copy, and how long it takes.

Targets (agree with the product owner). One-NGO proposal: recovery point 24 hours, recovery time 4 hours. A shared installation needs tighter targets and a database with point-in-time recovery.

A backup must hold: the database (provider backups plus your own pg_dump); uploaded files (provider versioning or a scheduled copy; the database holds only references); ENCRYPTION_KEY with its version history (without it restored secrets are unreadable); TOKEN_SECRET is not needed for data. If a free tier cannot meet the recovery point, schedule pg_dump to an encrypted bucket in the same region with limited access.

Drill, on a staging database with test data, timing each step:

  1. pg_dump --format=custom --no-owner --file=ngo-backup.dump "<source DATABASE_URL>" (keep the default, which includes grants and row-level-security policies; the platform depends on both).
  2. Create a fresh, empty database on PostgreSQL 15+.
  3. On the target server create the role first (pg_dump does not copy roles): create role app_runtime nologin if missing, grant app_runtime to <server user>, and create extension if not exists pg_trgm if allowed.
  4. pg_restore --no-owner --role=<target owner> --dbname="<target DATABASE_URL>" ngo-backup.dump. Errors about app_runtime or grants mean step 3 was missed.
  5. Run npm run deploy:prepare against the copy; it should apply no migrations.
  6. Start the server with the original ENCRYPTION_KEY and check: /health 200; /ready 200; a known member signs in (two-step too, which proves the key); a record list shows the same counts; an attachment downloads; a new receipt number continues with no gap or reuse; recent audit events are present.
  7. Record the time from step 2 to the last passing check and the age of the newest data against the agreed targets.
  8. Drop the target database and delete the local dump. Never leave a dump of personal data on a workstation.

Record the targets, measured restore time, data age, provider and tier, warnings, and the backup schedule now in place.

10. Footprint on the target environment

Measure memory, cold start and save latency on the F1 instance against the hosted database, with the database awake and after it has paused. Budgets (docs/documents.html#architecture section 13):

Measure Budget Local result 2026-10-25
Resident memory, peak with two concurrent script saves under 250 MB 144 MB
Cold start to the first signed-in API response under 4 s 4.7 s median, 3.0 to 9.9 s (missed; machine loaded)
Save latency, 95th percentile under 500 ms 18 ms create, 38 ms pay

A missed budget is reported, not fixed by the operator. Steps: deploy to staging with instance_class: F1, min_instances: 0; wait for no running instance, then time the first /health and first signed-in response (repeat five times, report median and range); repeat after the hosted database reaches its idle pause; read peak memory from the dashboard or appengine.googleapis.com/system/memory/usage; with the instance warm, create 200 donations and mark each paid from two clients and take the 95th percentile. npm run measure:bundle refuses a non-local database on purpose.

11. Payment gateway sandboxes

Goal, for each of Razorpay and Cashfree in test mode only: an order is created, a signed webhook marks the payment paid, a duplicate changes nothing, a forged webhook is refused.

Razorpay Cashfree
Create order POST https://api.razorpay.com/v1/orders, Basic auth (key id, key secret) POST https://sandbox.cashfree.com/pg/orders (production uses https://api.cashfree.com); headers x-client-id, x-client-secret, x-api-version: 2023-08-01
Webhook URL <PUBLIC_BASE_URL>/webhooks/payments/razorpay/<organisation code> <PUBLIC_BASE_URL>/webhooks/payments/cashfree/<organisation code>
Signature checked X-Razorpay-Signature: HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body with the webhook secret x-webhook-signature: base64 HMAC-SHA256 of x-webhook-timestamp + raw body with the client secret
Acts on payment.captured (paid), payment.failed (failed) SUCCESS (paid), FAILED (failed)
Duplicate key X-Razorpay-Event-Id, else event plus payment id Event type plus payment id

A valid webhook gets 200 {ok:true}; an invalid one 401 {ok:false}. The staging host must be reachable over HTTPS and allow outbound calls to the provider hosts. The app has no gateway setup screen or payment page yet, so credentials go through the admin route and payments are completed with the providers' own test tools.

Store credentials as a member with payments:configure:

  • PUT /api/admin/payment-gateways/razorpay with keyId, keySecret, webhookSecret (all required), 204.
  • PUT /api/admin/payment-gateways/cashfree with keyId (client id), keySecret (client secret), environment (sandbox or production, default sandbox), 204.
  • GET /api/admin/payment-gateways lists configured, enabled, keyId, environment, never a secret.

Secrets are encrypted under ENCRYPTION_KEY and never returned. Create a payment as a member with payments:create: POST /api/payments with recordId, amount, provider, and onPaid (the donation workflow step and field names; ask the development team for the shipped module's keys).

# Do Expected
1 Pay the order in the sandbox Webhook 200; payment paid; within seconds the donation is paid, a receipt is issued and the ledger posts
2 Resend the same webhook 200, nothing new: no second receipt or posting
3 Send a webhook with a wrong signature 401; nothing changes
4 A payment that fails Payment failed; donation not paid
5 A webhook whose amount differs from the order (if the sandbox allows) Not marked paid; audit entry payment.amount_mismatch
6 A webhook for an unknown organisation code 401

Repeat 1 to 4 for both providers. Report anything that differs from the providers' current documentation.
